Indonesia’s Low-Grade and High-Grade Nickel Matte Value Chain

Research cut-off: 17 September 2026 | V19 full report | 49 project entries, 84 buyer headings and 1,192 associated shipment records

01 Industry foundations, economics and operating cases

Research cut-off: 17 September 2026. The project register contains 49 entries. The supplied export sample contains 1,192 records dated 6 January 2023 to 29 August 2026. Shipment quantities are physical tonnes unless stated otherwise; company capacities and production often use contained nickel. The two measures are kept separate throughout.

1 The business is a chain of processing and commercial choices

Indonesia’s matte sector connects ore and NPI production, matte upgrading, and downstream refining. It is not an independent pool of capacity that automatically turns into exports. A producer may own a converter and choose not to run it; an exporter may ship high-grade matte without operating the conversion equipment itself.

HNMI illustrates the first point. Its high-grade matte output was zero in the second and third quarters of 2025. Production resumed in October after the business secured a better sales contract. By the second quarter of 2026, output continued but the conversion business reported a negative cash margin. CNGR requires two separate routes: low-grade feed, exemplified by Debonair records, can link to upgrading in China; Jade Bay, Zhongtsing and NNI also have high-grade-associated exports that instead require investigation of downstream refining. The group must not be represented as uniformly exporting low-grade feed for upgrading in China. Only high-grade matte created by low-to-high conversion in China is Chinese matte output. Imported Indonesian high-grade matte subsequently refined into sulphate remains Indonesian matte production. MBMA FY2025 presentation, p5; CNGR’s Qinzhou announcement.

This report distinguishes disclosed operating facts, shipment evidence, supported industrial interpretation, and unresolved points. An export proves a shipment under a named entity. It does not independently prove manufacture by that entity. Repeated sales to the same buyer do not establish a long-term contract, and a memorandum of understanding does not establish completed deliveries.

Captive supply also needs a precise definition. A controlled smelter selling through its group’s trading subsidiary is a group channel. Deliveries to a minority shareholder’s refinery are shareholder-related supply. Sales to an independent refinery are external industrial sales. A warehouse named as consignee may reveal logistics without identifying the owner or ultimate user. Ownership, contractual obligations and physical destinations must be examined separately.

2 The materials and the processing boundaries

Material Function in the chain Main analytical risk
NPI or ferronickel Nickel-bearing iron alloy, primarily feeding stainless steel; certain facilities can process it further into sulphidic intermediates Treating all RKEF output as matte
Low-grade matte Iron-rich sulphidic nickel intermediate, feeding converters or a specifically demonstrated direct-refining route Classifying material from a commercial name or price without composition and process evidence
High-grade matte Nickel-rich sulphidic intermediate after iron removal, feeding nickel salts or metal refining Calling it finished Class 1 nickel or assuming the exporter owns its conversion line
MHP Mixed nickel-cobalt hydroxide, commonly from the HPAL route Counting it as low-grade matte
Nickel sulphate Refined nickel salt used in precursor production and other industrial applications Confusing salt mass with contained nickel or assuming all batteries require nickel
Refined nickel High-purity metal used in alloys, plating and other markets Confusing plant output, exchange registration and actual deliveries

Grades vary by process and product. Many high-grade shipments in this sample are around or above 70% nickel, while some low-grade records are in the teens or twenties. These observations do not create a universal assay threshold. Buyers also evaluate sulphur, iron, cobalt, copper and other impurities. SMM’s refinery description, and the named Jinchuan and Guangxi Bamo facilities discussed later, provide a more useful link between feedstock and processing than a company name alone. SMM nickel refinery.

Three matte routes and one competing route

The basic RKEF route uses a rotary kiln and electric furnace to produce NPI. Suitable operations can modify their processes and use additional facilities to enter the sulphidic chain. Technical capability, completed modifications and actual operating choice are separate facts.

Low-grade and high-grade matte can be produced within one integrated site or in different legal entities. Hengjaya’s October 2022 announcement explicitly described low-grade output being upgraded in IMIP converters for a conversion charge. It did not name the conversion company. The statement establishes a processing arrangement, not a converter owned by Hengjaya. Nickel Industries, 3 October 2022.

CNGR adds a cross-border variant: Indonesian low-grade matte is shipped to Qinzhou, converted into high-grade matte and processed further. Its Indonesian facilities that produce high-grade matte locally must be treated separately. Group ownership cannot erase the country in which each conversion takes place. CNGR, 31 July 2023.

HPAL, or high-pressure acid leaching, is a parallel route from limonite to MHP and downstream nickel-cobalt products. It competes for investment and customers without requiring a matte stage. In June 2026 Nickel Industries explicitly preferred HPAL economics and carbon characteristics when abandoning its Oracle matte-converter investment. That is evidence of one company’s decision, not a universal assertion that HPAL is cheaper for every ore and project. Operating update, 17 June 2026.

Cobalt and double counting

Nickel-cobalt groups invest upstream partly because their existing refining and materials operations need secure feed. That does not establish cobalt recovery at every Indonesian matte plant. The export sample lacks shipment-level cobalt assays, so this report does not manufacture cobalt tonnages or by-product revenue allocations. MHP and the subsequent separation circuits are an important parallel cobalt chain.

Likewise, the same nickel must not be counted repeatedly as NPI capacity, low-grade matte capacity and high-grade matte capacity when calculating primary supply. Stage capacities can be listed to identify bottlenecks, but a converter is not necessarily a new source of mined nickel.

The user-confirmed Huake–Youshan route makes this concrete: Huake NPI goes to Youshan for low-grade matte production, then returns to Huake for high-grade conversion. This business intelligence is labelled separately from public evidence. The same two entities can be both seller and buyer, depending on the processing stage.

3 Why projects are built and why supply relationships persist

For materials and refining groups, upstream investment can improve control over feed availability, composition and delivery. For stainless and NPI investors, matte adds a route into another nickel market. For Indonesian resource groups, processing extends the business beyond ore or the original energy portfolio. The relevant corporate disclosures are examined in the group chapters; these motives should not all be reduced to battery demand.

Persistent supply relationships can arise from shareholder offtake obligations, compatible processing equipment, coordinated trading and financing arrangements, or an independent refinery’s need for qualified feed. The mechanisms are plausible, but they become a specific contractual fact only when named parties and disclosed terms support the connection.

A proper agreement register should identify the document, effective date, term, minimum quantities, pricing basis, quality conditions and evidence of performance. Where those terms remain private, the report says so. A recurring consignee is not evidence of a five- or ten-year exclusive contract.

The conversion margin matters more than a headline price spread

On a common recoverable-nickel basis, the extra net proceeds from selling high-grade matte must cover the NPI sales opportunity forgone, sulphidation and iron-removal costs, metal losses, energy, logistics and working capital. Different reference prices, quotation periods, currencies and payability factors complicate a simple comparison of LME nickel and an NPI quote.

A useful decision rule is therefore: net high-grade matte proceeds minus foregone NPI proceeds minus incremental processing, losses, logistics and financing costs must be positive. This is an explanatory framework, not a model populated with invented market assumptions. Internal transfer prices can also move profit between a mine, a smelter and a converter.

MBMA reported a second-quarter 2026 high-grade matte selling price of USD15,811/tNi, cash cost of USD16,354/tNi and cash margin of negative USD542/tNi. The one-dollar difference between subtraction of the rounded numbers and the reported margin is rounding, not a separate economic issue. The company linked higher conversion cost to higher NPI transfer prices. Production fell 12% quarter on quarter while sales rose 30%, demonstrating that inventory and dispatch timing can move sales differently from output. MBMA Q2 2026, pp1, 7 and 17.

High-grade matte can feed sulphate or metal refining, while sulphate producers can source MHP and other materials. Export growth therefore cannot be equated one-for-one with battery demand. The IEA’s 2026 outlook also separates mining, refining and end-use demand rather than treating an intermediate-product flow as final consumption. IEA 2026 outlook.

4 MBMA and HNMI provide a documented stop-and-restart case

Ownership and operating structure

MBMA acquired 60% of PT Huaneng Metal Industry on 31 May 2023 for USD75 million, with Tsingshan retaining 40%. The IMIP conversion facility had already been in steady production since 2022. The acquisition date is not its construction or first-production date. Historical company disclosure gives 50 ktNi/year of high-grade conversion capacity; the original spreadsheet’s 240,000 figure is not adopted as contained-nickel capacity. MBMA Q2 2023; 2023 annual report, acquisition note.

MBMA is an Indonesian listed nickel and battery-materials platform with the SCM mine, RKEF operations, HNMI, acid-related processing and HPAL interests. Buying an operating converter provided an earlier route to high-grade matte revenue and additional processing margin than building the entire chain from scratch.

Its upstream RKEF group consists of CSI, BSI and ZHN. The 2024 ownership chart lists 50.10% interests in each; CSI and BSI came under control through April 2022 capital increases, while ZHN completed commissioning in mid-2023. Their main product is NPI, but part of their production supplies low-grade matte to HNMI. MBMA 2024 annual report, p78; corporate milestones.

In Q2 2026, the reported 19,505 tNi RKEF production measure included 2,282 tNi in low-grade matte sold to HNMI. External NPI sales were separately stated at 17,223 tNi, with intragroup low-grade sales eliminated on consolidation. The company did not split the low-grade contribution among the three plants, so this report does not allocate it arbitrarily. MBMA Q2 2026, pp6 and 17.

The reason production changed is disclosed

HNMI produced 50,315 tNi in high-grade matte in 2024 and 19,998 tNi in 2025. Quarterly 2025 production was 9,525 tNi in Q1, zero in Q2 and Q3, then 10,473 tNi in Q4. Management attributed the suspension to a preference for more profitable NPI operations and the October restart to a more favourable sales contract. The reviewed presentation did not disclose the counterparty, term or price formula of that contract. It cannot be assigned to a particular customs buyer merely because that buyer later appears. FY2025 presentation, p5.

Q1 and Q2 2026 production was 10,361 and 9,165 tNi respectively. Q2 sales reached 10,457 tNi. Yet the supplied customs sample has no HNMI-heading shipments in January–July 2025 and records its 2025 shipments only in December. The company’s reported earlier production and sales prove that the customs sample is not a complete plant operating account.

Cumulative HNMI-heading shipments in the sample total 101,874.01 physical tonnes. Buyers include CNGR, Jinchuan, SMM, Glencore and trading or storage entities. Vinsen’s exact identity remains unresolved. The observed external customer base rules out describing HNMI as exclusively captive supply, but it does not rule out long-term contracts. What is missing is the public connection between named counterparties and detailed contractual terms.

5 Nickel Industries separates NPI offtake from matte marketing

Nickel Industries is an Australian listed company with Indonesian mining and processing assets. Its four RKEF projects, Hengjaya, Ranger, Angel and Oracle, were each 80%-owned at the 2025 reporting date. Hengjaya began NPI production in March 2019 and Ranger in May 2019. Angel’s lines and power plant ramped up in 2022. Oracle first produced NPI in November 2022 and entered commercial sales in 2023. Their current NPI operating status must be separated from historical matte plans. Operations; announcement archive.

The 2025 annual report states that Shanghai Decent committed to buy all NPI production under the collaboration arrangements, while non-NPI nickel products may be sold to third parties. The relationship combines ownership, construction and the industrial-park operating network. It does not identify the final plant receiving every tonne of NPI. Annual report, p25.

When Hengjaya switched in 2022, its low-grade matte was upgraded by IMIP conversion facilities. Sales of the upgraded product were contracted to an unnamed third party through March 2023 at payabilities linked to average SHFE nickel settlement prices. The company explained the choice through weaker NPI prices and more attractive matte pricing. Precise payability percentages were not disclosed. 3 October 2022 announcement.

A separate December 2023 announcement named Glencore AG as a direct matte buyer from January 2024 for an initial six-month term. Price and volume were confidential. Management explicitly described customer diversification into western markets. This is not evidence of an unchanged multi-year exclusive contract in 2026. 12 December 2023 announcement.

Angel’s March 2023 plan to modify two RKEF lines was conditional on market conditions and converter availability. Oracle’s USD15 million converter-investment option was subsequently abandoned in June 2026 as Nickel Industries favoured HPAL. These plans therefore cannot be counted as confirmed current high-grade output. Angel announcement; Oracle decision.

The four plants produced a combined 58,128 tNi in NPI in H1 2026 on a 100% project basis: Hengjaya 8,033, Ranger 8,243, Angel 19,295 and Oracle 22,557 tNi. Limited appearances in the matte export sample do not negate this NPI activity. Q2 2026 report, pp8 and 12.

6 New projects require separate evidence for purpose and delivery

Hanrui’s existing cobalt-powder, cobalt-salt, copper-cobalt and Ganzhou metal operations explain its move into nickel resources. The December 2023 venture disclosure assigned 70% to Hong Kong Hanrui and 30% to Huaxin Investments for a 20 ktNi/year high-grade matte project in the Huabao park, with approximately USD243.2 million of planned investment. The partner’s role included access to land, infrastructure and raw-material channels. These construction arrangements are not a disclosed downstream matte offtake contract. Huaxin Investments must not be confused with Huayou or the Huaxing project. Venture announcement; investment document.

The March 2026 completion-related disclosures and 10 April ignition describe different commissioning milestones. The route through low-grade and high-grade matte is supported, but sustained qualified production, actual sales and a named long-term buyer remain unconfirmed in the reviewed evidence. Group nickel revenue must not be assigned to the Indonesian project. Zhenshi announcement; Hanrui H1 report.

Dowstone’s June 2022 framework with Huadi explicitly sought nickel security and lower feed costs for its materials business. It proposed a 20,000-tonne high-grade matte project and investment by Dowstone of no more than RMB1 billion. Final project ownership, the interpretation of the capacity unit, completed commissioning and downstream offtake have not been closed by reliable project-level evidence. An industry table’s expected 2025 start is not an actual production announcement. PT Jiana Energy Resources in later filings cannot be merged with the proposed PT Dowstone entity without verification. 2022 annual report; framework announcement; 2026 H1 report.

ITSS, Guang Ching, LSI and SMI remain on the map because they have real industrial or shipment roles. Their matte exports alone do not establish additional matte production units. IMIP identifies LSI as an NPI producer and associates it with MBMA, but its exact equity chain has not been reconciled with the reviewed parent-company structure. Similar “Lestari” names must not be merged. Huashuo, Langit and Alam Cipta remain explicitly identified candidates or unresolved entities, rather than being assigned invented shareholders and customers. IMIP’s LSI profile.

02 CNGR, Weiming, Hengsheng and Chengtun

Research cut-off: 17 September 2026. Project capacities below refer to contained nickel where the source uses that basis. Physical shipment tonnes are not nickel tonnes. The main report supplies the reconciled customs totals; this chapter does not estimate missing exports or convert incomplete product descriptions into assays.

1. The processing location determines where high-grade matte is produced

CNGR’s July 2023 announcement establishes an actual route: material from its Indonesian Weda Bay base reached Qinzhou and fed the Chinese OESBF low-to-high-grade matte line, followed by nickel sulphate and precursor production. Indonesian low-grade matte production and Chinese high-grade matte production can therefore coexist within one supply chain. High-grade matte made at Qinzhou is Chinese production, not Indonesian production. Counting the nickel once at each stage and adding both amounts would double-count the same metal. CNGR, 31 July 2023

This also resolves the apparent conflict between a project named “high-grade matte,” low-grade matte exports and a battery-material end market. A project name describes an investment objective; the operating section and product shipped describe what happened at a particular date. Subsequent installation of Indonesian upgrading facilities does not erase the earlier China-based processing route. Zhongtsing and NNI are recognised below as Indonesian high-grade matte producers on evidence from their Indonesian plants, not on Qinzhou output. NNI announcement, 12 November 2024; CNGR H1 2026, p.13

Weiming illustrates a different downstream configuration. Antaike’s May 2026 site visit reported that Jiaman low-grade matte feeds Shengqing’s refining section without an intervening high-grade matte stage. This is a named field observation, although the complete process package, mass balance and batch-level procurement records remain private. It supports that particular route rather than a universal assumption about all low-grade matte customers. Antaike, 21 May 2026

2. CNGR connects upstream feedstock to a materials business

CNGR’s activities span battery materials, energy metals and recycling. Its stated reasons for Indonesian investment include raw-material security, higher self-supply and lower external purchasing costs. These are documented corporate objectives. A more specific claim that a particular shipment went to China because of electricity prices, sulphur costs or a temporary processing margin would require evidence that has not been disclosed. CNGR, 14 January 2023; CNGR, 31 July 2023

The importing entity is not necessarily the consuming plant. Hunan Zhengyuan, Guangxi Zhengyuan, Changsha Chuangyuan and Guizhou CNGR New Material Trading are trading platforms. Guangxi CNGR New Energy is a manufacturing entity. Hong Kong trading and investment companies must also remain separate. A Hunan purchaser does not establish Hunan as the processing destination, while a Singapore investment-company buyer does not establish Singapore refining capacity. The H1 2026 subsidiary schedule identifies these roles; the IPO document describes Hunan Zhengyuan’s international procurement and sales function. H1 2026, pp.164–169; IPO, company description

P02: PT Zhongtsing New Energy

Ownership over time. The 2021 arrangement was 70% CNGR and 30% RIGQUEZA. A disproportionate capital increase in December 2025 raised CNGR to 85%: its Hong Kong subsidiary subscribed US$195 million and RIGQUEZA US$9.9 million. Control existed before the increase. Historical intragroup classification therefore remains appropriate during the controlled period, but historical attributable capacity and earnings cannot all be calculated at 85%. H1 2021; 2025 financial report, PDF p.126, printed p.125

Operating route. The eventual high-grade matte project uses ore-to-low-grade matte side-blowing and a subsequent upgrading section. The early-2026 announcement and H1 report confirm that phase two connected both stages. Low- and high-grade matte production is consequently established. The corresponding 60,000-t-Ni upstream and downstream stages are not 120,000 tonnes of additional nickel capacity. Product allocation and utilisation still require operating data rather than a nameplate assumption. Phase-two announcement; H1 2026, p.13

Commercial interpretation. Sales to controlled CNGR trading companies are group supply channels. Sales to other groups, where documented in customs records, must be identified separately. No disclosed enforceable buyer-specific offtake text was obtained for annual minimum volumes, pricing benchmarks, payability, take-or-pay obligations, exclusivity or duration. Vertical integration explains a commercial purpose; it does not establish a fixed-price long-term contract.

P03: PT Nadesico Nickel Industry, NNI

Acquisition and control. CNGR subsidiaries acquired 60% and an employee ownership platform acquired a separate 7% in 2023. The combined 67% should not be attributed entirely to the listed company. The accountants’ report dates the group’s 60% acquisition to 1 July 2023; H1 2026 still shows 60%. Pre-acquisition trades need their own relationship classification. References to Delong describe the former ownership background and do not by themselves undo CNGR’s subsequent control. Business disclosure, PDF p.74; Accountants’ report, printed I-136; H1 2026, p.169

High-grade matte is supported by plant events. CNGR reported converter heating on 27 October 2024, feeding on 5 November and successful high-grade matte production on 6 November. It identified ferronickel, low-grade matte and high-grade matte capabilities, with a full-line high-grade matte target of 40,000 t Ni annually. This is evidence of Indonesian upgrading and output, not merely a project label. The eventual target is not proof that all lines reached full utilisation at first production. CNGR, 12 November 2024

The 80,000-t-Ni low-grade matte project designation must not become 80,000 tonnes of high-grade matte capacity. Multiple product options may improve flexibility between stainless and battery markets; that is a reasonable commercial interpretation, not proof of a particular monthly switch. CNGR trade buyers establish a group channel, but not whether the final output of each parcel became nickel sulphate or nickel cathode. Buyer-specific long-term purchasing terms remain undisclosed in the reviewed materials.

P18 and P19: Debonair and Jade Bay

The historical control gap can now be closed. CNGR’s 2022 annual report dates the acquisition of Debonair Holdings and investment in Jade Bay to 18 July 2022, with an accounting acquisition date of 31 July based on operational and decision-making control. Hong Kong Zhongtuo owns all of Debonair Holdings, which owns 50.1% of PT Debonair Nickel Indonesia. Zhongtuo separately obtained 50.1% of Jade Bay. Thus 2023 transactions with controlled CNGR purchasers need not remain “historical relationship unknown.” 2022 annual report, business combinations

The plants have distinct milestones. Debonair’s base project started construction in November 2021. On 12 January 2023, furnace 48 tapped; furnace 47 had been ignited. The announcement described subsequent sections and an eventual 27,500-t-Ni low-grade matte target. A furnace tap is not simultaneous completion of every matte section. Jade Bay was separately expected to start in early Q2 2023 and cannot inherit Debonair’s January first-tap date. CNGR, 14 January 2023

Jade Bay’s original scope was two RKEF lines and 27,500 t Ni of low-grade matte, with 50.1% Zhongtuo and 49.9% DNPL. DNPL is not interchangeable with Debonair Holdings. CNGR’s later NNI announcement identifies Weda Bay as an already established overseas high-grade matte base. That supports base-level production, but the publicly reviewed evidence does not fully allocate its downstream upgrading facilities between the two legal plants. Confirm the separate low-grade operations and count shared base-level high-grade capability once until plant-level allocation is supported. Jade Bay agreement, 8 July 2022; NNI announcement

The Weda Bay-to-Qinzhou route is directly documented at base level, without a full split between exporters. The 2022 investment terms also gave CNGR a put-back right if its partners failed to meet production commitments. This protects an equity investment; it is not an offtaker’s minimum purchase commitment. It must not be converted into a sales contract or assumed price formula. 2022 report

3. Weiming links environmental equipment, Indonesian smelting and Wenzhou refining

Weiming’s established activities include environmental operations and equipment. Its nickel investment expands into materials rather than representing a traditional nickel miner developing an owned orebody. The company explicitly positioned Indonesian intermediates as feedstock support for its Wenzhou battery-material project; it also supplies equipment to overseas materials projects. Raw-material integration and equipment opportunities are therefore documented strategic connections. Weiming’s 2022 project introduction; H1 2026 management discussion

P09: PT Jiaman New Energy

The 70% and 63% figures describe different levels. Jiaman was established on 24 March 2022 with 70% held by Weiming Singapore and 30% by Merit. The listed company held 90% of the Singapore vehicle through Wenzhou Jiawei, giving 63% attributable ownership in Jiaman. A separate investment partnership held the remaining 10% upstream. Consequently, 70% to 63% is not evidence of a seven-percentage-point sale. Direct ownership and attributable economic ownership need separate columns. 11 May 2022 announcement

Operating progress must be staged. The overall design is 40,000 t Ni of high-grade matte. The first 10,000-t-Ni side-blown line entered equipment commissioning in Q4 2024. In April 2025, management confirmed qualified high-grade matte after the low-to-high process. The Q3 report subsequently recorded a commercial sales licence obtained in July. H1 2026 records trial production of the third side-blown line at the end of June. These dates represent commissioning, successful product, commercial authorisation and incremental capacity; none should replace the others. April 2025 investor record; Q3 2025 report; H1 2026, p.12

Successful upgrading does not imply that every tonne must be upgraded in Indonesia. The May 2026 field report describes direct low-grade matte supply into Shengqing refining. A producer can possess high-grade capability while selling low-grade material to a customer configured to use it. The reviewed evidence does not quantify the resulting energy or total-cost saving, so no numerical saving is assumed. Antaike

What is actually public about offtake? The April 2023 strategic arrangement provided priority high-grade matte supply to Shengqing after completion, rather than exclusive purchase of all output. Q1 2023 report A Wenzhou signing report in April 2024 described an intended minimum of 10,000 t Ni that year, through Wenzhou Jiazhan, with US$126 million involved. These are agreement targets, not verified deliveries. They do not establish a realised price or payable percentage, and the subsequent production and licence dates prohibit treating the signing as proof of 2024 output. The contract’s adjustment provisions, delays, penalties, payment schedule and any later low-grade substitution clause were not obtained. Wenzhou, 15 April 2024

Shengqing is not a consolidated captive subsidiary. Weiming holds 54.55%, but its H1 2026 report states that governance arrangements prevent control of significant operating decisions. Shengqing is an equity-accounted associate, outside consolidation. Jiaman-to-Shengqing may be described commercially as upstream self-supply, but the transaction classification should be related-party supply, not the same category as consolidated intragroup transfers. The reported related-party sales also include equipment and cannot be used as a matte-only revenue number. H1 2026, pp.186,195

Procurement changed with the plant’s development. In March 2025, management said Shengqing mainly purchased high-grade matte from Indonesian producers and would consider Jiaman supplies once available. By August 2026, a named Shengqing commercial executive described low-grade matte bonded warehousing, staged customs release and deferred tax payment as ways to reduce working-capital and port-delay pressures. That is direct evidence for a logistics decision. The article does not identify the exporting plant, so its cargo cannot automatically be assigned to Jiaman. March investor record, Q12; Wenzhou Evening News, 3 August 2026

4. P48 Hengsheng: confirmed low-grade output, unconfirmed high-grade operation

Hengsheng is in the Huadi area of Bantaeng, South Sulawesi. CNGR’s January 2026 shareholder-loan disclosure identifies Hainan Riseco at 50%, Huadi at 20%, CNGR at 15% and SHENGWEI at 15%. CNGR is an investor, not the controlling parent. Purchases by CNGR entities should therefore be classified as shareholder-linked sourcing rather than consolidated captive supply. The parties’ involvement connects project development, local industrial infrastructure and downstream materials, but it does not establish an exclusive customer or additional control rights. 23 January 2026 disclosure, PDF p.5

The clear first-production event is low-grade matte tapped on 28 August 2023. A shareholder-sourced project account describes drying and rotary-kiln preparation followed by oxygen-enriched side-blowing, with a first-phase 10,000-t-Ni target. It supports ore-to-low-grade matte, not automatic high-grade output. Hainan Riseco account, 20 September 2023

A 2025 on-site internship report offers useful supplementary terminology: its printed p.7 defines the plant’s “Luppen FeNi” as low-grade nickel matte; p.1 describes an upgrading converter under construction for roughly 75% Ni material. The placement ran from March to August 2025 and the report was approved in October. It is observational supporting evidence, not a listed-company filing. It cannot prove the grade of every 2026 “NUGGET FENI” shipment or establish that a converter remained unfinished in 2026. Internship report, pp.1,7

No reviewed source establishes a Hengsheng high-grade first heat, sustained production or a named high-grade assay order. SMGA ore cooperation and shareholder financing do not fill that gap. Nor was direct Hengsheng low-grade matte feeding into an electrolytic-nickel route established. The practical classification remains low-grade matte in production; high-grade upgrading under development in historical evidence, actual output unconfirmed. Offtake minimums, price formulas and exclusivity remain unknown. Shareholder disclosure; on-site report

5. Chengtun Guizhou is a downstream manufacturing customer

Chengtun combines resources, smelting and trading. Chengtun Energy Metal Chemical (Guizhou), held 52.84% at year-end 2025, is a smelting entity distinct from Chengtun New Energy Materials (Guizhou). Its Indonesian intermediate purchases should be analysed as production feedstock unless the transaction evidence shows resale. 2025 annual report, subsidiaries and operations

The government-published 2025 modification assessment describes internally produced high-nickel matte and externally purchased high-grade matte entering hydrometallurgy, acid leaching, extraction and crystallisation for nickel sulphate. An electrolytic-nickel option was planned for weak sulphate sales. Process compatibility is documented, but the plan alone cannot prove operation of that new line or the destination of every parcel. Cross-group suppliers should remain external feedstock sources, while an intermediary requires further tracing to the receiving plant. Environmental assessment, printed p.2

Chengtun’s planned 40,000-t-Ni ChengMach project must not appear as operating captive supply. The company decided on termination and winding-up on 25 December 2025. Cancellation does not remove its Chinese refinery’s feedstock demand; it removes the assumption that ChengMach provides that demand. Actual sourcing from Youshan, Zhonghe or external producers requires trade and company evidence rather than a calculation from old project capacities. 2025 audited report, termination note

6. Related projects remain future supply until operation is demonstrated

CNGR’s Xingqiu, Xingquan and Xingxin have identified Indonesian legal entities and 70% ownership in H1 2026. Xingquan and Xingxin each retain a 40,000-t matte project under construction. Construction balances alone cannot exclude every trial operation, but without named operating evidence they should not enter the producing list. Xingqiu’s absence from the same pair of construction entries does not establish start-up. H1 2026, pp.126,165

Weiming’s 40,000-t Jiamanda and 50,000-t Jiamanmei are separate developments, not aliases for Jiaman’s operating lines. The H1 2026 unpaid-capital disclosure and the rating report’s project schedule support retaining them as planned. Tax incentives are not production evidence. The combined 130,000-t headline is not installed operating capacity. Jiamanda’s 60% ownership is also not Shengqing’s current stake or Jiaman’s attributable interest. H1 2026, pp.184–185; 2026 rating report

7. A consistent classification for the main trade matrix

Observed relationship Classification What remains to be established
Controlled Zhongtsing/NNI/Debonair/Jade Bay sells to a CNGR platform after control begins Consolidated group supply channel Final consuming plant and processing route
Hengsheng sells to CNGR Shareholder-linked procurement Grade by shipment, final route, contract terms
Jiaman sells to Shengqing Related-party supply to an associate Actual fulfilled quantity and pricing terms
An Indonesian producer sells to Chengtun Guizhou Production feedstock; relationship checked by counterparties Whether intermediary, related-party or independent sale
A forwarder or logistics company appears as consignee Logistics channel Economic buyer and final consumer

The largest distortions arise from moving Chinese processing into Indonesian production, treating a trading platform as a refinery, confusing minority investment with control, or replacing current products with an investment title. Once those errors are removed, undisclosed contract details can remain honestly unknown without rejecting well-supported production and trade evidence.

8. Matched-period exports: production events provide context, not automatic causation

The consolidated user-provided customs dataset compares January–July in both years. All figures below are physical tonnes, not contained nickel or complete plant production. Source: the main report's the accompanying export workbook.

Plant Jan–Jul 2025 Jan–Jul 2026 Supported interpretation
Zhongtsing 30,416.2 46,825.2 Higher captured exports coincide with phase-II and conversion commissioning; the full increase cannot be attributed to expansion alone.
NNI 19.66 24,311.0 The comparison starts from one extremely small shipment; an enormous percentage would mislead.
Jiaman 0 16,474.0 Captured shipments started after July 2025; zero in this window does not mean no exports during the full year.
Jade Bay 0 6,315.3 A change in shipment timing, not evidence of first production in 2026.
Hengsheng 20,295.7 8,797.1 Lower captured physical exports; no evidence yet identifies a production cut, high-matte switch or closure as the cause.

Zhongtsing's additional conversion stage is a documented industrial event. Its effect should be examined alongside shipment assays: a shift from low- to high-grade matte changes physical tonnage per unit of contained nickel. Physical export growth is therefore not equivalent to nickel-supply growth.CNGR H1, p13

NNI first produced high-grade matte in November 2024, so the 2026 increase must not be described as a 2026 start-up. The dataset contains only one 19.66-tonne shipment in January–July 2025, but 7,069.2 tonnes across that full year. Coverage, shipping schedules and ramp-up may each affect the comparison; their contributions cannot be separated from the available disclosures.NNI commissioning

Jiaman's first observed 2025 shipment is dated 3 August; full-year captured exports total 19,587.8 tonnes. That timing is consistent with its July commercial-sales licence, without proving licensing was the sole cause. Its third side-blowing line entered trial production at the end of June 2026, too late to explain all shipments earlier that half-year.Weiming Q3; Weiming H1, p12

Jade Bay already appears in the dataset on 13 March 2024. Its 2025 records begin on 18 August, explaining the zero January–July comparison without implying zero production. Likewise, Debonair's missing later records do not establish closure; declaration-entity changes within the Weda Bay base remain a question to investigate.

Hengsheng still has captured trade on 29 August 2026. Recent original descriptions say NUGGET FENI under HS720260 and contain no assay. Low-grade matte is an analytical classification, not a direct chemical declaration on every ticket. Ore supply, maintenance, stocks, customer changes and declaration changes remain possible explanations for lower recorded exports, with none established. The product conclusion remains: confirmed low-grade matte production; high-grade matte production unconfirmed.

03 CNGR exporter products and Chinese high-grade matte refineries

Debonair (P18) is the low-grade-feed example in this sample. It records 60,117.51 tonnes in 2023 and 21,044.16 tonnes in 2024, or 81,161.67 tonnes in total. Some purchases use CNGR trading entities and Qinzhou discharge, while Tsingshan, Chengtun and unresolved buyers also appear. The entire quantity cannot be called Qinzhou furnace feed. CNGR’s July 2023 disclosure links Weda Bay feed to Qinzhou conversion at base level, without identifying the legal producer of every shipment. CNGR, 31 July 2023

Jade Bay (P19) exports high-grade matte in the supplied records. Its 31 records in 2024–2026 total 20,996.11 tonnes, supported by nickel-matte descriptions and original assays around or above 70% Ni. Seven CNGR-buyer records total 4,484.42 tonnes; other buyers include Walsin, GEM, Jinchuan and Huayou channels. Material already exported as high-grade matte requires investigation of downstream refining, salts or metal, not another assumed low-to-high stage. The exported product still does not independently establish ownership of the production equipment.

Zhongtsing (P02) changes its observed product mix over time. Low-grade candidates appear in 2024 and earlier 2025 records. By 24 October 2025, a shipment explicitly states 71.48% HIGH NICKEL MATTE. High-grade samples reach 15,114.40 tonnes in 2025 and 44,281.84 tonnes in 2026. Another 13,884.18 tonnes in 2025, around 66–69% Ni with conflicting HS and LUPPEN FENI descriptions, remain separate. The disclosed integration of the Indonesian conversion stage is consistent with this change. It cannot be represented as a permanently low-grade exporter relying entirely on China for upgrading. Phase-II announcement

Seven 2026 records totalling 8,190.47 tonnes in the old low-grade-candidate group require particular care: their original assays are 40.33–45.20% Ni and the buyer is Weiming Shengqing, with Wenzhou discharge. Five descriptions say NICKEL SULFIDE and two say LUPPEN FENI. This review retains them as nickel intermediates requiring further product identification, rather than automatically typical low-grade matte. They are not CNGR captive Qinzhou feed. Calling them intermediate-grade matte or assigning direct wet refining requires specifications or process evidence.

NNI (P03) has independently confirmed high-grade production and high-grade-associated export records. The sample totals 37,522.84 tonnes across 31 records. Generic NICKEL MATTE descriptions lack original assays, so assumed grades are not laboratory results. CNGR’s announcement confirms first high-grade production on 6 November 2024. CNGR, SMM and Huayou buyers appear, preventing an assumption that every shipment returns to CNGR in China. First-production announcement

Absence from this sample does not prove a product never existed. Low-grade capability, high-grade capability, exported product and actual buyer remain separate fields.

CNGR also purchases high-grade matte from external suppliers

Besides Zhongtsing, NNI and Jade Bay, the sample identifies HNMI, Huake, Walhsu, Westrong, Lancon, Cosan, USMI and Nicole supplying high-grade-associated material to CNGR buyer channels. Chinese refining feed therefore includes group and external procurement. The following cumulative quantities cannot be allocated directly to a particular Qinzhou or Guizhou production line.

CNGR’s Chinese high-grade nickel matte consumers: legal entities, processing stages and operating evidence

Research date: 17 September 2026. Conclusions apply to named facilities. A process available somewhere in the group cannot be attributed to every base. Imported high-grade matte can enter Chinese refining directly; it would be incorrect to describe all Indonesian feed as low-grade matte requiring conversion in China.

1. Qinzhou: high-grade matte refining preceded low-to-high conversion

CNGR’s announcement dated 8 April 2022 names its southern base and records high-grade matte trial feeding on 17 February, qualified nickel sulphate crystal output on 6 April, and the start of batch production that day. By publication, qualified output had continued for three days. The company also identifies the first Indonesian high-grade matte shipment arriving at the base and continuing sulphate supply to its precursor workshops. This directly demonstrates that Qinzhou could receive Indonesian material already in high-grade matte form, before the 2023 OESBF low-to-high commissioning. The later process must not be imposed retrospectively on all shipments since 2022. Company announcement, 8 April 2022

Page 3 of Guangxi CNGR’s 2024 factory due-diligence report identifies the legal entity and location: the China–Malaysia Qinzhou Industrial Park in the Qinzhou Port Area of the China (Guangxi) Pilot Free Trade Zone, east of Luhai Avenue and north of Danshuiwan Avenue. This document establishes the facility’s identity but does not separately enumerate high-grade matte feed; it does not replace the 2022 operating evidence. Factory report, February 2025, PDF page 3

The July 2023 company announcement confirms low-grade matte conversion through OESBF and its connection to sulphate and precursor production. This added an upstream processing option alongside the earlier direct high-grade matte route. It does not establish that all CNGR Indonesian plants export only low-grade matte. Company announcement, 31 July 2023

The 2025 sustainability report again names Guangxi CNGR and Qinzhou and confirms the linked OESBF and MSP refining chain. High-grade matte refining into sulphate is therefore supported. Monthly feed quantities, the proportions of purchased versus locally converted high-grade matte, and individual Indonesian suppliers still require receiving records and feed ledgers. Identifying a trading buyer such as Guangxi Zhongwei Zhengyuan does not establish that all its purchases went to Qinzhou. 2025 sustainability report, published 31 March 2026, green-factory case study

The 2024 material-change approval, 自贸钦审批环〔2024〕54号, describes a built but not yet environmentally accepted high-grade matte line proposed for expansion and an increase in MSP sulphate capacity from 60,000 to 80,000 contained-nickel tonnes/year. These are approval status and revised capacities, not actual annual production or proof of completed acceptance. Direct access to the government PDF failed; government search-index text was available, and that access limitation remains relevant. Government document, indexed text from PDF page 2

2. Tongren: the refining entity is Guizhou CNGR Resource Recycling

The Guizhou Environmental Engineering Assessment Centre’s opinion 黔环评估书〔2022〕101号 places the project at the junction of Main Roads 2 and 1 in Dalong Economic Development Zone and names Guizhou CNGR Resource Recycling Industry Development Co., Ltd. as developer. Page 2 describes low-grade matte upgrading through oxygen-enriched side blowing, followed by high-grade matte grinding, leaching and extraction into nickel sulphate for sale to CNGR Advanced Material Co., Ltd. for precursor production. Page 10 reiterates the developer’s identity. The design figure of 807,600 tonnes/year of nickel sulphate solution is physical solution tonnage, not contained nickel. Downloading the government PDF timed out; this review used searchable text at the government URL, and a complete local copy remains to be obtained. Guizhou government assessment, 2022, printed pages 2 and 10

Operating status can be assessed beyond the 2022 groundbreaking announcements. The 2023 annual report separately identifies the western-base 80,000-contained-nickel-tonne matte-refining sulphate project as “high-to-sulphate.” The 2024 annual report states that 2024 was its first operating year and that ramp-up had not reached full capacity. This supports an operating high-grade matte refining stage. It does not automatically establish that the original upstream low-to-high stage was commissioned at the same scale, or that design capacity equals actual high-grade matte consumption. 2023 annual report, construction in progress; 2024 annual report, proceeds-funded project performance

The latest qualification is material: PDF page 23 of the 2026 interim report states that the Guizhou 80,000-contained-nickel-tonne sulphate project changed its feed structure in consideration of overall operating economics, resulting in production below expectations. It does not identify the replacement materials or their shares. A shift to MHP, low-grade matte or cessation of high-grade matte use cannot be invented. The supported description is an established high-grade matte refining project with an undisclosed current feed mix. 2026 interim report, PDF page 23, independently checked in local M01

3. Ningxiang and Kaiyang: materials production or research does not establish commercial matte consumption

Hunan CNGR New Energy holds patent CN113584314B, covering an atmospheric-pressure high-grade matte leaching method and nickel sulphate. The application was filed on 29 July 2021 and granted on 14 March 2023; the address is 42 Chuangxin Road, Ningxiang Economic Development Zone. This establishes research by the named entity. It does not establish industrial operation, commercial capacity or consumption of a particular Indonesian shipment. Patent, PDF page 1

The company’s initial environmental disclosure for Ningxiang phase VI in 2021 identifies precursor and recycling pilot workshops producing ternary precursors and nickel, cobalt and manganese sulphate solutions. That disclosure does not specifically identify high-grade matte feed. Combined with the patent, it remains insufficient to confirm commercial consumption. The most useful next evidence would be a commercial line’s environmental raw-material table, actual feed during acceptance monitoring, or a named commissioning announcement. Initial disclosure, 3 August 2021

Kaiyang’s reviewed investment project is 200,000 tonnes/year of iron phosphate, and the 2026 interim report discusses its performance as an iron-phosphate project. Combining the group’s four Chinese bases with its group-level high-grade matte purchases would not establish consumption at Kaiyang. High-grade matte use remains unconfirmed; this is also not proof that such use is impossible or absent. 2026 interim report, PDF page 23

4. The Shaodong English buyer name: a Chinese trading entity is identified, but matching still needs an address

PDF page 4 of the 13 March 2024 credit document identifies 邵东市中伟新材料有限公司, unified social credit code 91430521MA7JAFFX8L, incorporated on 15 February 2022 and wholly owned by Hunan Zhongwei Zhengyuan New Material Trading Co., Ltd. Its registered address is Room 102 of the commercial building at Zhongwei Gongguan, Shaodong. The 2026 interim report describes its business as product sales and import/export trading. “Shaodong Cngr Advanced Material Co., Ltd.” is therefore a candidate match, but the English spelling alone does not complete registration matching. A buyer ticket cannot establish a Shaodong facility processing low-grade matte. 2024 credit document, PDF page 4; 2026 interim report, subsidiary table

Research conclusions

CNGR’s Indonesian exports must be classified by project, date, original description and assay. Jade Bay shipments already identified as high-grade matte cannot be assumed to require low-to-high conversion after arrival in China.

The Chinese processing network includes both imported high-grade matte entering refining and imported low-grade matte entering a separately demonstrated conversion stage. A particular receiving base remains unassigned where plant-level delivery evidence is missing.

Qinzhou and Tongren have evidence supporting actual high-grade matte refining. Ningxiang remains unconfirmed at the commercial-feed level, while Kaiyang cannot inherit a process from group-level disclosures. Zhengyuan, Chuangyuan and Shaodong trading entities must remain separate from processing plants.

High-grade matte produced by low-to-high conversion in China is Chinese output. Indonesian high-grade matte subsequently refined into nickel sulphate does not create another increment of high-grade matte production.

04 Huayou, Walsin, Harum and the Tsingshan network

This chapter reflects evidence reviewed through 17 September 2026. Stated capacity means the design capacity in the cited document, not actual annual output. Export quantities use the consistent main-scope aggregation of the supplied shipment records and are physical tonnes. User-confirmed commercial information is distinguished from publicly documented equipment, ownership and production facts.

1. How to read this industrial network

These facilities are not simply a collection of independent high-grade nickel matte plants. Ore reduction into nickel pig iron, sulphidation into low-grade nickel matte, and conversion into high-grade nickel matte can occur within one facility or across separate legal entities. High-grade matte subsequently undergoes hydrometallurgical leaching and purification to supply nickel sulphate or high-purity nickel, connecting the Indonesian smelting chain with battery precursors and other nickel markets.

Equipment, actual production, customs shipments and buyer ownership therefore require separate treatment. RKEF equipment demonstrates a rotary-kiln/electric-furnace reduction stage; it does not alone establish low-grade matte production. Converter procurement establishes an investment, not necessarily commercial output. A customs declaration establishes a shipment under an entity’s name, not that every tonne was produced by that entity. Trading subsidiaries may handle title, settlement and resale without operating the final processing facility.

Three organisational models emerge. Huake and Youshan participate in a cross-plant processing route. Sunny and Walhsu represent upstream production and high-grade conversion within the Walsin-controlled network. Westrong has particularly clear evidence of an integrated low-grade-to-high-grade matte route within its own facility. Lancon and Cosan retain exposure to both stainless-steel feedstock and matte markets, although their individual operating configurations are less fully disclosed.

2. Why the sponsors built these businesses

Huayou Cobalt is a listed Chinese metals and battery-materials company spanning nickel and cobalt feedstocks, nickel salts, refined nickel, precursors and cathode materials. Indonesian production supports its manufacturing system in China and overseas. Huake’s pyrometallurgical matte and Huayue/Huafei’s hydrometallurgical MHP are different feedstocks produced by different entities. Group-wide nickel-intermediate capacity must not be represented as matte capacity. Huayou Holding is also distinct from listed Huayou Cobalt: Youshan’s historical 29.25% Huayou-related interest belonged to the holding-company network. [S1–S2]

Chengtun Mining expanded its resource and metallurgical business into Indonesian nickel through Youshan. RKEF production can serve stainless steel through NPI or the battery-nickel chain through low-grade matte. Walsin Lihwa, with established wire, cable and stainless-steel businesses, developed WNII for upstream NPI security and used Sunny to enter battery-nickel feedstocks. Walsin’s own annual report connects its matte investment with the battery-materials market; that does not imply that all matte is consumed in its Taiwanese stainless-steel furnaces. [S3–S4]

Harum Energy diversified from coal into nickel mining and processing. Infei provides NPI exposure, Westrong high-grade matte, and separate HPAL investments MHP. Co-investment links Harum and Walsin but does not place the two listed groups under common control. Tsingshan contributes industrial parks, processing experience, infrastructure and international trading networks. These connections matter commercially, but location in a Tsingshan-associated park does not establish a specific ownership relationship. S5

Jindal Stainless is an Indian stainless-steel producer. Its Cosan investment was framed around NPI supply security. Cosan should therefore not be interpreted exclusively as a battery-material plant. Equally, Jindal’s annual report describes both NPI and nickel matte manufacturing, so an NPI-only description would omit part of its disclosed business. S10

3. Huake and Youshan: cross-plant cooperation without double-counting

Huake’s ownership and operating history

PT Huake Nickel Indonesia is located in IWIP. Historical project disclosures describe a 70% Huayou and 30% Tsingshan-related structure. This is a construction-period ownership reference, not a substitute for verification in every later year. Huayou’s 2020 feasibility materials specify four submerged electric furnaces and four P-S converters, including drying, calcination, reduction and sulphidation/conversion. Design documents establish the intended configuration, not a simultaneous commissioning date for every unit. S1

The production milestones are stronger. The high-grade matte line entered trial production in the fourth quarter of 2022, with the first shipment to China at the end of November. Huayou’s 2023 interim report said the 45,000-tonne nickel project had essentially reached design capacity, and its annual report continued that description. The 2025 report still lists Huake among completed pyrometallurgical projects. High-grade matte production is consequently established; the remaining questions concern feedstock allocation and product switching, not whether Huake possesses a functioning matte business. [S1–S2]

Youshan’s project name differs from its actual product stage

PT Youshan Nickel Indonesia was established on 15 August 2018. Although investment documents call it a 34,000-tonne nickel high-grade matte project, the 2024 transaction valuation explicitly identifies its actual product stage as ferronickel/NPI and low-grade matte. Its four RKEF lines include four drying kilns, four calcining kilns and four electric furnaces, supported by a 250 MW power station and port facilities. The valuation records actual investment of USD443 million. Trial production began in September 2020 and planned capacity was reached in 2021. The project’s headline name is not evidence of a separate high-grade matte finishing stage at Youshan. S3

Chengtun’s 2025 annual report continues to describe production of ferronickel or low-grade matte using RKEF, with downstream smelters converting the latter into high-grade matte. This is more specific than the project title: Youshan is a confirmed NPI/low-grade matte producer. No new direct evidence was obtained for a separate high-grade converter commissioning at Youshan itself. S3

Ownership changed; commercial cooperation may continue

Initially, Chengtun’s subsidiary Hongsheng International held 55% of Huawei Nickel, which held 65% of Youshan. This gave Chengtun an indirect 35.75%. The Huayou Holding network held the remaining 45% of Huawei, equivalent to 29.25% of Youshan. The other 35% of Youshan belonged to a Tsingshan-associated investment network. That project-level interest should not be confused with a fully traced ultimate economic interest without further ownership documents.

On 12 August 2024, Hongsheng signed an agreement to acquire Huayou Holding Hong Kong’s 45% of Huawei for approximately USD162.8 million. Chengtun’s 2024 annual report states that Hongsheng subsequently held 100% of Huawei and indirectly 65% of Youshan. New trade must therefore not be explained using the superseded Huayou-related 29.25% interest. Continuing supply cooperation is a commercial relationship, not proof that the former shareholding persisted. S3

The user-confirmed physical route

The research adopts the user-confirmed route: Huake NPI → Youshan low-grade matte → Huake high-grade matte. This specific cross-plant relationship is industry information supplied by the user. Public documents independently support Huake’s upstream reduction and high-grade conversion capabilities, and Youshan’s actual low-grade matte production for downstream processing. These facts are compatible, but no public contract was obtained that simultaneously specifies all three links, allocation percentages, processing charges, transfer of title or contract duration.

The route must not be described as covering all Youshan output or all Huake high-grade matte. Nor can the contractual model be labelled outright sale, tolling or processing on consignment without documentation. Production totals must count the nickel once, rather than adding the NPI, low-grade matte and high-grade matte stages as three separate supplies.

Specialisation is the commercial interpretation: one facility provides reduction and final conversion capabilities while another undertakes the low-grade stage. Any cost advantage depends on transport, sulphidation, oxygen availability, recoveries and market conditions. The existence of a route does not quantify its savings. The 2024 ownership change does not itself invalidate continued operational cooperation.

4. Sunny and Walhsu: two stages under Walsin control

Ownership and development

Walsin approved the acquisition of 50.1% of Sunny for USD200 million in April 2022. Its 2025 annual report states that the matte lines acquired in the second half of 2022 entered trial production at year-end and full production in the first quarter of 2023. Walsin’s aggregate interest increased to 79.6125% in January 2024, rounded to 79.61% in its financial statements. Walsin Singapore directly held 50.10% of Sunny and 75% of Berg, which in turn held 39.35% of Sunny: 50.10% + 75% × 39.35% = 79.6125%. This is the economic interest across two ownership paths, not a single entity’s direct shareholding. The structure appears on PDF pages 28–29 of Walsin’s third-quarter 2024 financial statements. S9 Harum’s first-quarter report dates THN’s acquisition of approximately 10.55% of Sunny to 24 January 2024. Direct and indirect holdings must be distinguished. [S4–S5]

Walhsu is controlled by Sunny; earlier financial statements recorded Sunny at 99.9% and WNII at 0.1% as Walhsu’s direct legal shareholders. The 99.9% figure is not Walsin’s ultimate look-through interest; see PDF page 125 of its third-quarter 2024 financial statements. S9 Walsin’s 2023 report identifies a Walhsu high-grade matte converter equipment contract with Eternal Tsingshan dated 26 June 2023, worth approximately USD49.33 million. Subsequent capital investment also concerned the high-grade line. The equipment contract is a construction milestone, not the date of first qualified output. A precise Walhsu commercial commissioning date was not obtained. S6

Equipment and actual products

Sunny’s factory due-diligence report published in March 2026 covers March 2025–February 2026. Its Chinese equipment description lists four 60,000 kVA electric furnaces, four rotary kilns, four drying kilns and an air-granulation system, and identifies NPI and low-grade matte as products. The English translation incorrectly calls the rotary kilns “converters.” It must not be used to invent four additional high-grade converters at Sunny. S7

Older Harum descriptions refer to Sunny’s investment business more broadly as RKEF plus conversion facilities and high-grade matte. Taken with Sunny’s own equipment report and Walhsu’s named procurement, the appropriate legal-entity interpretation is confirmed NPI/low-grade matte at Sunny and high-grade conversion at Walhsu. High-grade sales by the Sunny business do not require all conversion assets to be registered at Sunny itself.

Buyers and contractual limits

Sunny or Walhsu sales to Walsin Singapore or Walsin Lihwa are group channels, not wholly independent external sales. Walsin Singapore has investment and financing functions; no Singapore matte-leaching plant was established by this research. Its appearance as buyer does not demonstrate final consumption in Singapore, or direct use of matte in Taiwanese stainless steel.

Huayou entities, Guangxi B&M and Jingmen GEM are customers outside Walsin’s controlling group on the ownership evidence reviewed. Hong Kong Rui Pu is identifiable as a Tsingshan trading channel: Lygend’s 2026 Shenzhen exchange response explicitly places Rui Pu and Golden Harbour International Pte. Ltd. under the same ultimate controller as Tsingshan. That does not make Rui Pu part of Walsin’s group. A particular shareholder-linked offtake arrangement still requires evidence.

No complete, publicly named long-term offtake contracts were obtained for these Sunny/Walhsu buyer relationships. Duration, minimum liftings, allocation percentage, pricing coefficient and exclusivity remain undisclosed in the reviewed material. Repeated shipments must not be converted into an invented five- or ten-year contract.

5. Westrong: integrated conversion, changing shareholder channels

Harum’s 2024 first-quarter report explicitly describes four RKEF lines producing low-grade matte for four converters producing high-grade matte, with approximately 56,000 tonnes of contained nickel annual capacity. Westrong’s own 2026 due-diligence report confirms RKEF and converter facilities and commercial production from early 2024. Harum records first matte sales in March 2024, including sales to an Indonesian refined-nickel producer. This links the processing chain with actual commercial activity. [S5, S8]

Ownership must be dated. Walsin approved its 29.5% investment in 2022 and approved a disposal in August 2023. Its 2024 third-quarter financial statements state that the entire interest was actually sold in January 2024. Harum’s HNI increased its project holding from 20% to 80.7% on 26 January 2024. Approval in 2023 was not completion, and the historic Walsin holding cannot be carried forward indefinitely. [S5, S9]

Sales to Walsin during the holding period can be described as shareholder-channel business. After disposal, they are cross-control-group commercial sales, with a historical investment-network qualification where relevant. Harum’s concurrent minority interest in Sunny does not put Harum and Walsin under common control. Formal accounting related-party classification should follow the applicable period’s financial statements.

Low-grade matte is an internal intermediate at Westrong. Its existence does not create a second equivalent block of merchant low-grade matte capacity. Harum’s 2026 first-half update also reports maintenance affecting RKEF output in the second quarter: commercial operation does not mean uninterrupted full utilisation. Buyer-specific minimum volumes, duration and pricing remain unavailable. The unnamed Indonesian refiner must not be relabelled as one of the Chinese buyers in the trade dataset.

6. Lancon and Cosan: stainless-steel security and matte flexibility

Industry tables often combine the two IWIP projects. Legal names should govern identification, including Chinese transliteration variants for Lancon. Jindal’s directly evidenced 49% interest is in Cosan, not automatically in Lancon.

Jindal’s original cooperation concerned development, construction and operation of an NPI smelter. Its 2024/25 annual report, records the 49% Cosan investment through wholly owned Sungai Lestari from 17 April 2023. PDF page 410 describes Cosan’s business as NPI/nickel matte manufacturing and records operating revenue for the year ended March 2025, whereas the comparative year shows none. This establishes both the disclosed product scope and commercial business, but does not split revenue or production by product. PDF page 279 describes the acquisition structure. S10

A January 2026 Guomao Futures operating table identifies the combined Lancon/Cosan route as RKEF plus converters, commissioning in January 2025 and operating, with combined high-grade matte capacity of 40,000 tonnes contained nickel. This total must not be assigned independently to both plants. January 2025 is the phase-commissioning milestone recorded in that industry table, not the earliest shipment date. The supplied sample already records 4,915.02 physical tonnes for Lancon and 350.55 tonnes for Cosan in 2024. Those earlier shipments do not by themselves establish own-produced volumes or the date on which each plant’s converters entered commercial operation. Industry operations evidence and shipments support a high-grade business while preserving NPI production or switching. Individual low-grade merchant output, converter asset ownership and current product mix remain less fully established. S11

Jindal disclosures also authorise related-party transactions between Cosan and Sungai Lestari or Jindal’s Dubai subsidiary. Annual transaction limits are corporate authorisations, not guaranteed purchases or long-term take-or-pay contracts. Minimum liftings, exclusivity and complete contract durations were not obtained. Jindal’s stainless-steel feedstock rationale must not be replaced with an assumption that all Cosan production serves battery markets.

7. Related NPI and developing projects

WNII supports Walsin’s stainless-steel raw-material position. Its 2025 annual report confirms NPI production and sales. A small interest in Walhsu does not establish low-grade matte production at WNII.

Infei’s factory report and Harum’s website identify two RKEF lines and commercial operation from April 2022, with 28,000 tonnes of contained nickel NPI capacity. Harum’s ownership of Westrong does not convert Infei into a matte producer. Andalan has a historical Huayou plan to modify acquired NPI operations toward matte, but the latest reviewed materials do not establish completion or actual matte production. These plants remain NPI assets or conversion-watch candidates, not automatically dedicated low-grade suppliers. S12

Huaxing at IPIP requires a separate development category. A named refractory contractor confirms the P05 pilot line’s oxygen-enriched side-blown smelting furnace and reduction-furnace lining. The core smelting furnace was ignited in March 2026, described as entry into production preparation. A separate P07 pyrometallurgical construction contract under the same named Huaxing Indonesian entity was disclosed by China National Chemical Engineering in April 2026. P05 ignition does not demonstrate full P07 completion; P07 construction does not prove P05 inactivity. The two codes cannot be added as independent capacity without a scope reconciliation. Qualified high-grade product output was not established in the reviewed evidence. S15

The Huafа/Huashuo candidate should remain distinct from Huaxing. Similar Chinese names or group labels are not enough to identify an Indonesian operating entity. Without a verified legal name, equipment and commissioning evidence, it remains a candidate project and must not enter confirmed operating low- or high-grade matte capacity.

8. Why the buyers purchase matte

Huayou Hong Kong is a trading/wholesale subsidiary, and Zhejiang Huayou headings may represent central procurement. Neither uniquely identifies the final processing plant. Quzhou Huayou Cobalt New Material produces refined nickel; Huayou New Energy Technology (Quzhou) is a different entity and should not automatically be labelled a matte-leaching plant.

Guangxi Huayou New Material has refined-nickel facilities. Guangxi B&M has a directly evidenced high-grade-matte-to-nickel-sulphate line: Huayou’s 2024 interim report, PDF page 26, states that the line with 50,000 tonnes contained nickel annual capacity passed its self-organised acceptance review in December 2023. This provides a concrete reason for matte procurement, rather than inference from cathode-material production alone. Both companies belong to Huayou but remain separate legal entities and facilities. S13

Jingmen GEM disclosed an oxygen-storage project for high-grade matte leaching, and GEM describes nickel sulphate production at Jingmen. This supports a leaching-to-nickel-salts use case, potentially feeding precursors, without proving the destination of every shipment. Rui Pu and Golden Harbour Pte. are trading channels rather than demonstrated final leaching plants. Golden Harbour International Limited without “Pte.” must not be merged automatically: exchange disclosures identify a Hong Kong property subsidiary with that exact name, requiring address or registration matching. [S13–S14]

Commercial feasibility depends on nickel grade, sulphur and impurities, recoveries, shipping terms, processing charges, nickel-sulphate and refined-nickel prices, and the customer’s own production plan. These are economic mechanisms, not clauses recovered from private contracts. Processing capability does not establish an obligation to buy all output from any Indonesian supplier.

9. Export changes are not equivalent to production changes

The consistent January–July comparison shows Huake exports of 21,377 tonnes in 2025 and 21,129 in 2026; Walhsu 9,697 and 4,465; Westrong 19,284 and 15,091; Lancon 18,352 and 11,754; and Cosan 4,482 and 12,744. These are physical shipment tonnes, not contained nickel, and cannot be added to design capacity or used as utilisation rates.

Westrong’s disclosed second-quarter maintenance provides one operational explanation compatible with weaker shipments, but does not quantify how much of the export change it caused. Its documented Indonesian domestic sales also demonstrate why export data can omit part of commercial deliveries. No equivalent evidence was obtained to attribute Walhsu’s decline or the contrasting Lancon/Cosan changes to production cuts, a shift between plants, particular customers or a new contract. Those remain hypotheses. Huake’s near-flat exports likewise do not establish unchanged production.

Sunny has no low-grade matte exports in the supplied 2025/2026 database. This does not contradict its factory report: domestic delivery for conversion can fall outside an export dataset. The dataset alone cannot determine how much was transferred to Walhsu or another converter.

10. What is established and what remains undisclosed

Youshan’s low-grade product and Huake’s high-grade capability are established; the user-confirmed cross-plant route is included as user-supplied operational information. Sunny’s NPI/low-grade production must be separated from Walhsu conversion. Westrong has the clearest integrated internal low-grade-to-high-grade route. Lancon/Cosan retain NPI and matte flexibility without invented plant-specific merchant low-grade tonnage.

The material remaining gaps are cross-plant supply/processing contracts, current plant-specific Lancon/Cosan ownership and configuration, Walhsu’s precise first-production date, and buyer-specific offtake terms. Customs data can test changes in buyers and shipped products; it cannot replace those documents or turn successive processing stages into additional nickel supply.
